What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t want to make a big thing out of it, but I’ve been having trouble at my apartment. I’m pretty sure some of the people there are watching when I come and go, and I think they’re putting notes about me to the office. Everybody keeps saying they’re just regular notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel that way to me. I thought maybe I should come talk to somebody about it… if that’s alright.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like a voice talking to me in the room, if that’s what you mean. Sometimes I’m listening pretty hard because of the apartment stuff, but I haven’t noticed voices when nobody’s there. I mean, I haven’t hit my head or had anything like that happen recently, not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling that people at your apartment are watching you or putting notes about you to the office?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s been… a little over a year now. Does that answer it right?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself losing interest in seeing people or doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have, some. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I used to look forward to choir practice, playing bridge, cooking beans on Sunday, doing little repair jobs for folks… things like that.\n\nLately I mostly just let the phone ring, and I tell myself there’s no point in going. I know that probably doesn’t sound too good, but that’s kind of how it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Have other people told you that your speech or behavior has seemed disorganized or hard to follow?\n\nPatient: A couple people have said I’m hard to follow sometimes, yeah. I don’t mean to be. I don’t want to overstate it, but that heavy “what’s the point” feeling, and me pulling back from things… that’s been going on about a year and a couple months now.\n\nDoes that answer it right?\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when the apartment-watching beliefs were still going on for at least two weeks, but you were not feeling depressed during that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there was. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there were a few weeks where I was doing a little better mood-wise — eating a bit better, not waking up with that hopeless feeling so much.\n\nBut even then, I still thought the neighbors were watching my door and, you know… reporting on me somehow. That part didn’t really let up just because I wasn’t as depressed. Yeah… sometimes, I think. I don’t want to say I’m always hard to follow, because I can explain things fine if it’s simple.

I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I used to look forward to choir practice, playing bridge, cooking beans on Sunday, doing little repair jobs for folks… things like that.\n\nLately I mostly just let the phone ring, and I tell myself there’s no point in going. I know that probably doesn’t sound too good, but that’s kind of how it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Have other people told you that your speech or behavior has seemed disorganized or hard to follow?\n\nPatient: A couple people have said I’m hard to follow sometimes, yeah. I don’t mean to be. It’s just when I try to explain things, I might start talking about the rent, then somehow I’m on the marina, then the choir robes, and then the wires in the walls… and folks look at me like I skipped half the story.\n\nI’m not trying to confuse anybody. It makes sense in my head at the time, but I guess it doesn’t always come out right. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think so. I try not to call it that, you know, maybe it’s just me being tired or getting older, but… most mornings I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling. Like the day already went bad before I even get my feet on the floor.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it, but that’s how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed like that nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think so. More than two weeks, honestly. I don’t want to overstate it, but that heavy “what’s the point” feeling, and me pulling back from things… that’s been going on about a year and a couple months now.\n\nDoes that answer it right?\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when the apartment-watching beliefs were still going on for at least two weeks, but you were not feeling depressed during that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there was. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there were a few weeks where I was doing a little better mood-wise — eating a bit better, not waking up with that hopeless feeling so much.\n\nBut even then, I still thought the neighbors were watching my door and, you know… reporting on me somehow. That part didn’t really let up just because I wasn’t as depressed. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: For at least one month, have these experiences been present most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, not every single minute, but those suspicious thoughts about the neighbors and the office watching me… they’ve been around most of the time for about a year and a month now.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the kind of answer you mean, but it’s been longer than a month, yeah.\n\nDoctor: During the time since these problems began, have you been depressed for most of that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say so. I mean, I’ve had some days where I could talk a little clearer or act more normal, I guess, but most of the time since I moved, I’ve still felt pretty low.\n\nNot trying to make it sound dramatic, but I’ve felt kind of useless, and I haven’t really been interested in the things I used to like. So… yes, most of that time, I think. Is that the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when the beliefs about your neighbors continued for at least two weeks while you were not feeling depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there was a stretch like that. I don’t want to overstate it, because I wasn’t exactly, you know, feeling great or anything, but for several weeks I was doing a little better — eating more, not waking up with that heavy hopeless feeling so much.\n\nBut the thing with the neighbors… that was still there. I still believed they were watching my door and reporting on me somehow. So, yes, I think it did keep going even when I wasn’t feeling depressed like that. Is that answering it right?\n\nDoctor: Were the suspicious beliefs ever present for at least 2 weeks when you were not feeling depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. There was a spell of a few weeks where I wasn’t waking up feeling so hopeless, and I was eating a bit better, so I guess I wasn’t depressed the way I had been.\n\nBut the neighbor thing was still there. I still believed they were watching my door and somehow reporting on me. I know that probably sounds… not great, but yes, it was still happening even when my mood was better. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: When the beliefs about your neighbors are at their strongest, are you usually feeling depressed at the same time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… usually, yes. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but when I’m really down — like in that hollow kind of place, not eating much and not even answering the phone — that’s when I’m the most sure the apartment people are tracking me or talking to the office about me.\n\nSo… yes, I think they do kind of get strongest at the same time. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Since these problems started, have you been depressed for most of the time overall?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like every single minute was awful, because some days I could talk a little better or seem more clear.\n\nBut since I moved, most of the time overall I’ve felt pretty low… kind of useless, and not really interested in the things I used to care about. I guess I kept trying to act like I was okay, but if I’m being honest, it’s been there more often than not. Is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your emotions feel flat or blunted, like your face or voice don’t show much feeling even when something matters to you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I haven’t really noticed that, to be honest. I mean, maybe I get quiet sometimes, but I don’t know if my face or voice are flat like you mean.\n\nNobody’s really said that to me, at least not that I remember. I might just seem tired or reserved, maybe. Is that an okay way to answer it?\n\nDoctor: Outside of times when you felt depressed, did you still have trouble speaking clearly enough that other people said you were hard to follow?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes, I think. I don’t want to say I’m always hard to follow, because I can explain things fine if it’s simple.\n\nBut there are times, even when I’m not especially depressed, where I start trying to explain something and it comes out… all mixed together. Like I’ll start with the rent, then somehow I’m talking about the marina, then the choir robes, and then the wires in the walls, and people look at me like I skipped half the story.\n\nI guess that means yes, at least sometimes. Is that what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think… yes, there was a stretch like that. I don’t know if I counted it exactly right, but it was several weeks at least.

I was doing a little better in some ways — eating a bit more, not waking up feeling quite so hopeless every morning — so I wouldn’t say I was as depressed then.

But the other stuff was still there. I still thought the neighbors were watching my door and reporting on me. And when I tried to explain it, I don’t think I was always coming out clear.

Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a full month when the suspicious beliefs and mixed-up speech were still going on, but you were not depressed?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, there was a stretch like that. I don’t know if I counted it exactly right, but it was several weeks at least.\n\nI was doing a little better in some ways — eating a bit more, not waking up feeling quite so hopeless every morning — so I wouldn’t say I was as depressed then.\n\nBut the other stuff was still there. I still thought the neighbors were watching my door and reporting on me. And when I tried to explain it, I don’t think I was always coming out clear.\n\nDoes that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually energetic or needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way, anyway.\n\nI mean, I’ve had nights where I didn’t sleep well, or maybe I got a little more done than usual, but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually energetic like I didn’t need sleep. Mostly if I’m up, I still feel tired.\n\nI hope that answers it the right way."
